(dynamic workflow)","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"design","description":"Grant or revoke Claude agent access to your Design projects","argumentHint":"consent | revoke","builtin":true},{"name":"design-sync","description":"Push a React design system to claude.ai/design. This runs a converter that bundles the real component code (from Storybook or a bare package) and uploads it. Use when the user runs /design-sync or says \"sync my design system to Claude Design\".","argumentHint":"[<project hint, e.g. \"Acme DS\">]","builtin":true},{"name":"dataviz","description":"Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"update-config","description":"Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"verify","description":"Verify that a code change actually does what it's supposed to by exercising it end-to-end and observing behavior — drive the affected flow, not just tests or typecheck. Run before committing nontrivial changes; bootstraps this repo's project verify skill if none exists yet. Don't invoke it on a diff that only touches tests, docs, or other code with no runtime surface to drive (a change to product source always has one) — there's nothing to observe.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"debug","description":"Enable debug logging for this session and help diagnose issues","argumentHint":"[issue description]","builtin":true},{"name":"code-review","description":"Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs (plus reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups where the model's review recipe covers them) at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.","argumentHint":"[low|medium|high|xhigh|max|ultra] [--fix] [--comment] [<pr#>|<branch>|<path>]","aliases":["review"],"builtin":true},{"name":"simplify","description":"Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.","argumentHint":"[<target>]","builtin":true},{"name":"batch","description":"Research and plan a large-scale change, then execute it in parallel across 5–30 isolated worktree agents that each open a PR.","argumentHint":"<instruction>","builtin":true},{"name":"fewer-permission-prompts","description":"Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"doctor","description":"Health-check the user's Claude Code setup and fix issues: diagnose installation health — what the `claude doctor` terminal diagnostics cover — from local data (duplicate or leftover installs, PATH, unparseable settings files, broken or colliding agent definitions, skills whose frontmatter fails to parse); find unused skills, MCP servers, and plugins versus their context cost and disable dead weight; deduplicate local CLAUDE.md files against checked-in ones; trim checked-in CLAUDE.md files by cutting content a session could derive from the codebase (directory layouts, tech-stack lists, architecture overviews) while keeping gotchas, rationale, and non-standard conventions; migrate always-loaded CLAUDE.md guidance into lazy skills and nested CLAUDE.md files; flag slow hooks and context-heavy extensions; check the installed version is current; make auto mode the default permission mode; and pre-approve frequently denied read-only commands. Use when the user asks for a doctor run, checkup, audit, tune-up, or cleanup of their Claude Code setup or configuration.","argumentHint":"","aliases":["checkup"],"builtin":true},{"name":"loop","description":"Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). usage","argumentHint":"","builtin":true},{"name":"anthropic-skills:built-in-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses the built-in browser, the browser pane inside the Claude desktop app (also called the in-app browser, the browser pane, Claude's browser, or \"your own browser\"), whose tools are named mcp__Claude_Browser__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser tool instead. It covers the pane's persistent sign-ins, tabs and preview_start, reading pages as text, site approvals, what the pane cannot open, and what to do when it cannot be reached. It is not for Claude in Chrome (mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["built-in-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:chrome-browser","description":"Read this skill before the first step that uses Claude in Chrome, the browser extension whose tools are named mcp__claude-in-chrome__* (also called Chrome, the browser extension, or the external browser) and which acts in the person's real Chrome with their own sign-ins; before those tools are turned on there may be a single enable__mcp__claude-in-chrome tool instead. It covers loading the tools in one ToolSearch call, checking the person's open tabs and working in a new tab, site permissions, GIF recordings, console logs, dialogs to avoid, and when to stop and ask. It is not for the built-in browser (mcp__Claude_Browser__* or mcp__remote-devices__Claude_Browser__* tools), which has its own skill, and it does not decide which browser to use. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["chrome-browser"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:computer-use","description":"Read this skill before the first step of any request to do something in an app on the person's own computer (Notes, Finder, System Settings, any desktop app), to look at their screen, or for \"computer use\". Computer use (desktop control) lets Claude take screenshots of the person's desktop and control it with clicks, typing and scrolling through the Claude desktop app; its tools are named mcp__computer-use__* when the session runs in the desktop app and mcp__remote-devices__computer_* when a cloud session is linked to the person's computer; before computer use is turned on for a conversation there may be no such tools, only an enable__mcp__remote-devices__computer tool, which turns it on. It covers turning it on, picking the right tool, the access flow, and the safety rules for tiered apps, links and financial actions. It is not for websites, which go through Claude in Chrome or the built-in browser and their own skills. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["computer-use"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:deep-research","description":"Use this skill when the user's prompt requires (1) researching a topic across multiple sources, comparing options or alternatives, analyzing trends or history, understanding markets or industries, or reviewing literature or studies and (2) synthesizing that research into a comprehensive, narrative report. If you're planning to search the web or internal knowledge bases, consider using this skill. This skill coordinates research subagents, so use it only when you have a tool for spawning subagents (the Agent or Task tool); otherwise, research the question directly. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":""},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docs","description":"docs (living docs people share, comment on and edit; use only when the user asks for one: names a doc, document, page, memo, spec, PRD, runbook or write-up, asks for somewhere to share or keep editing something, or says yes to your doc offer; a plan, comparison, summary or notes asked in chat stays in chat (at most a one-line doc offer); a report, status update, recap or \"something I can send them\" with no form named → ask first: reply, doc or file?; tabs hold tables and live charts too; a pasted claude.ai/code/artifact/… link may be a doc: check with docs tools first; not HTML pages, apps or plain chat answers; a .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/PDF asked for by name → that format's skill): asked for one → no docs-connector instructions in context? call the docs connector's `guide` with topic.instructions first, then create the doc (headings only, no body) before any search, file read or plan, even with files attached. Documenting code means docstrings or repo docs, not a doc. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docs"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:docx","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx) or Word templates (.dotx). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file (to download, email or print), use this skill. However, if they ask for a document, page, report, memo, or notes WITHOUT naming a file format and the session offers Claude's own dedicated document or page skill or connector, use that instead. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or coding unrelated to document generation. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["docx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:import-memory","description":"Import a memory export from another AI assistant into Claude's memory — conversationally, additively, and with the content treated as data. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["import-memory"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:morning","description":"Render the user's morning brief as a styled HTML artifact, or set it up as a recurring weekday task. Use only when the user explicitly asks to run, see, or set up their morning brief, or if they invoke /morning by name. A question about their day, schedule, or calendar is not by itself a request for the brief; answer it directly instead. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["morning"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pdf","description":"Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pdf"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:pptx","description":"Use this skill any time a .pptx or .potx file is involved in any way — as input, output, or both. This includes: creating slide decks, pitch decks, or presentations as PowerPoint (.pptx) files; reading, parsing, or extracting text from any .pptx or .potx file (even if the extracted content will be used elsewhere, like in an email, summary, or creating a different type of slide deck); editing, modifying, or updating existing presentations; combining or splitting slide files; working with templates (.potx), layouts, speaker notes, or comments. Trigger whenever the user asks for a PowerPoint or .pptx file, or references a .pptx or .potx filename, regardless of what they plan to do with the content afterward. However, when the user asks for a deck, slides, a slide deck, or a presentation without naming a file format, default to using a dedicated slide-deck artifact type or a separate slides skill if this session offers one; otherwise, use this skill. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["pptx"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:setup-writing-style","description":"Learns how the user writes from their own sent messages and docs, and builds a voice profile so future drafts sound like them instead of generic AI. The profile is saved as the my-writing-style skill. Use when the user asks to set up, learn, or capture their writing voice, or complains that drafts sound generic or unlike them and no my-writing-style profile exists. Only for drafting text the user will send as themselves, not for Claude's own replies. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["setup-writing-style"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:skill-creator","description":"Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["skill-creator"]},{"name":"anthropic-skills:xlsx","description":"Use this skill any time a spreadsheet file is the primary input or output. This means any task where the user wants to: open, read, edit, or fix an existing .xlsx, .xlsm, .xltx, .csv, or .tsv file (e.g., adding columns, computing formulas, formatting, charting, cleaning messy data); create a new spreadsheet from scratch or from other data sources; or convert between tabular file formats. Trigger especially when the user references a spreadsheet file by name or path — even casually (like \"the xlsx in my downloads\") — and wants something done to it or produced from it. Also trigger for cleaning or restructuring messy tabular data files (malformed rows, misplaced headers, junk data) into proper spreadsheets. The deliverable must be a spreadsheet file. Do NOT trigger when the primary deliverable is a Word document, HTML report, standalone Python script, database pipeline, or Google Sheets API integration, even if tabular data is involved. (claude.ai sync)","argumentHint":"","aliases":["xlsx"]}],"uuid":"30b328bd-34eb-4917-bbb6-e2b8cc72d1e5","session_id":"f71484da-9cca-4504-a5c2-f8e1c65217d4"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018US6DbXEKAdB6fAPJ2QArd","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/**\n2\t * @module HealthController\n3\t * @description Health check endpoints for liveness and readiness probes.\n4\t *\n5\t * Extends StandardHealthController to inherit:\n6\t *   - @Public() decorator: bypasses TenantGuard + RolesGuard (critical for\n7\t *     Docker healthcheck which sends plain wget without auth headers)\n8\t *   - @SkipThrottle(): prevents rate-limiting of probe requests\n9\t *   - Consistent response format across all microservices\n10\t *   - Proper HTTP 503 when all checks fail (not just body status)\n11\t *\n12\t * Adds Redis and NATS connectivity as additional readiness checks\n13\t * beyond the standard database check.\n14\t *\n15\t * @see ADR-012 section 10 (Observability)\n16\t */\n17\timport { Controller, Inject, Logger, Optional } from '@nestjs/common';\n18\timport { InjectDataSource } from '@nestjs/typeorm';\n19\timport { DataSource } from 'typeorm';\n20\timport { ClientProxy } from '@nestjs/microservices';\n21\timport { StandardHealthController } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/health';\n22\timport Redis from 'ioredis';\n23\timport { REDIS_CLIENT } from '../shared/redis.provider';\n24\t\n25\t@Controller('health')\n26\texport class HealthController extends StandardHealthController {\n27\t  private readonly healthLogger = new Logger('messaging-service:HealthController');\n28\t\n29\t  constructor(\n30\t    @InjectDataSource() dataSource: DataSource,\n31\t    @Optional()\n32\t    @Inject(REDIS_CLIENT)\n33\t    private readonly redis?: Redis,\n34\t    @Optional()\n35\t    @Inject('NATS_SERVICE')\n36\t    private readonly natsClient?: ClientProxy,\n37\t  ) {\n38\t    super(dataSource);\n39\t    this.serviceName = 'messaging-service';\n40\t  }\n41\t\n42\t  /**\n43\t   * Additional readiness checks for Redis and NATS connectivity.\n44\t   * These supplement the standard database check from StandardHealthController.\n45\t   *\n46\t   * Optional dependencies that are not injected (undefined) are silently\n47\t   * skipped rather than reported as errors. This avoids false 'degraded'\n48\t   * status when the provider is simply not in the current module scope.\n49\t   *\n50\t   * A failing Redis or NATS check results in 'degraded' status (HTTP 200),\n51\t   * not 'not_ready' (HTTP 503) -- only total failure of ALL checks triggers 503.\n52\t   */\n53\t  protected override async getAdditionalChecks(): Promise<Record<string, 'ok' | 'error'>> {\n54\t    const checks: Record<string, 'ok' | 'error'> = {};\n55\t\n56\t    // Redis connectivity check (only if Redis client is injected)\n57\t    if (this.redis) {\n58\t      try {\n59\t        await this.redis.ping();\n60\t        checks['redis'] = 'ok';\n61\t      } catch (error) {\n62\t        this.healthLogger.warn(\n63\t          `Redis health check failed: ${(error as Error).message}`,\n64\t        );\n65\t        checks['redis'] = 'error';\n66\t      }\n67\t    }\n68\t\n69\t    // NATS connectivity check (only if NATS client is injected)\n70\t    if (this.natsClient) {\n71\t      try {\n72\t        await this.natsClient.connect();\n73\t        checks['nats'] = 'ok';\n74\t      } catch (error) {\n75\t        this.healthLogger.warn(\n76\t          `NATS health check failed: ${(error as Error).message}`,\n77\t        );\n78\t        checks['nats'] = 'error';\n79\t      }\n80\t    }\n81\t\n82\t    return checks;\n83\t  }\n84\t}\n85\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f71484da-9cca-4504-a5c2-f8e1c65217d4","uuid":"1188b3c9-5b4c-423d-997a-2029eac196c5","timestamp":"2026-09-19T14:30:06.907Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-e340d37a0d97/apps/messaging-service/src/health/health.controller.ts","content":"/**\n * @module HealthController\n * @description Health check endpoints for liveness and readiness probes.\n *\n * Extends StandardHealthController to inherit:\n *   - @Public() decorator: bypasses TenantGuard + RolesGuard (critical for\n *     Docker healthcheck which sends plain wget without auth headers)\n *   - @SkipThrottle(): prevents rate-limiting of probe requests\n *   - Consistent response format across all microservices\n *   - Proper HTTP 503 when all checks fail (not just body status)\n *\n * Adds Redis and NATS connectivity as additional readiness checks\n * beyond the standard database check.\n *\n * @see ADR-012 section 10 (Observability)\n */\nimport { Controller, Inject, Logger, Optional } from '@nestjs/common';\nimport { InjectDataSource } from '@nestjs/typeorm';\nimport { DataSource } from 'typeorm';\nimport { ClientProxy } from '@nestjs/microservices';\nimport { StandardHealthController } from '@aquaculture/backend-common/health';\nimport Redis from 'ioredis';\nimport { REDIS_CLIENT } from '../shared/redis.provider';\n\n@Controller('health')\nexport class HealthController extends StandardHealthController {\n  private readonly healthLogger = new Logger('messaging-service:HealthController');\n\n  constructor(\n    @InjectDataSource() dataSource: DataSource,\n    @Optional()\n    @Inject(REDIS_CLIENT)\n    private readonly redis?: Redis,\n    @Optional()\n    @Inject('NATS_SERVICE')\n    private readonly natsClient?: ClientProxy,\n  ) {\n    super(dataSource);\n    this.serviceName = 'messaging-service';\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Additional readiness checks for Redis and NATS connectivity.\n   * These supplement the standard database check from StandardHealthController.\n   *\n   * Optional dependencies that are not injected (undefined) are silently\n   * skipped rather than reported as errors. This avoids false 'degraded'\n   * status when the provider is simply not in the current module scope.\n   *\n   * A failing Redis or NATS check results in 'degraded' status (HTTP 200),\n   * not 'not_ready' (HTTP 503) -- only total failure of ALL checks triggers 503.\n   */\n  protected override async getAdditionalChecks(): Promise<Record<string, 'ok' | 'error'>> {\n    const checks: Record<string, 'ok' | 'error'> = {};\n\n    // Redis connectivity check (only if Redis client is injected)\n    if (this.redis) {\n      try {\n        await this.redis.ping();\n        checks['redis'] = 'ok';\n      } catch (error) {\n        this.healthLogger.warn(\n          `Redis health check failed: ${(error as Error).message}`,\n        );\n        checks['redis'] = 'error';\n      }\n    }\n\n    // NATS connectivity check (only if NATS client is injected)\n    if (this.natsClient) {\n      try {\n        await this.natsClient.connect();\n        checks['nats'] = 'ok';\n      } catch (error) {\n        this.healthLogger.warn(\n          `NATS health check failed: ${(error as Error).message}`,\n        );\n        checks['nats'] = 'error';\n      }\n    }\n\n    return checks;\n  }\n}\n","numLines":85,"startLine":1,"totalLines":85}}}

ts-node\nimport { basename, dirname, join, relative, resolve } from 'node:path';\n\nimport ts from 'typescript';\n\nimport {\n  collectFiles,\n  filterFilesBySnapshot,\n  isArchivedWorkspacePath,\n  normalizeWorkspacePath,\n  readWorkspaceFile,\n  requireScanRoots,\n  resolveInsideWorkspace as resolveAdapterPath,\n  workspacePathExists,\n} from './adapter-fs';\n\ntype FindingRule =\n  | 'high_risk_source_without_adjacent_test'\n  | 'migration_without_test'\n  | 'security_source_without_security_test';\n\ninterface AdapterInput {\n  readonly roots?: readonly string[];\n  readonly allowlist?: readonly string[];\n  readonly includeWriteBoundaryFindings?: boolean;\n  readonly repo_snapshot?: { readonly allowed_paths?: readonly string[]; readonly snapshot_hash?: string; readonly repo_state_id?: string };\n}\n\ninterface EvidenceRef {\n  readonly path: string;\n  readonly line?: number;\n}\n\ninterface AdapterObservation {\n  readonly id: string;\n  readonly type: string;\n  readonly path?: string;\n  readonly line?: number;\n  readonly name?: string;\n  readonly details?: Record<string, unknown>;\n}\n\ninterface AdapterFinding {\n  readonly id: string;\n  readonly rule: FindingRule;\n  readonly severity: 'medium' | 'high';\n  readonly path: string;\n  readonly line?: number;\n  readonly message: string;\n  readonly evidence: readonly EvidenceRef[];\n  readonly confidence?: number;\n  readonly actionability?: 'actionable' | 'review_required';\n  readonly review_reason?: string;\n  readonly details?: Record<string, unknown>;\n}\n\ninterface MemoryCandidate {\n  readonly belief_id: string;\n  readonly claim: string;\n  readonly confidence: number;\n  readonly evidence_refs: readonly string[];\n  readonly source_tool_id: string;\n}\n\ninterface AriaOutput {\n  readonly observations: readonly AdapterObservation[];\n  readonly findings: readonly AdapterFinding[];\n  readonly read_paths: readonly string[];\n  readonly evidence_sources: readonly string[];\n  readonly belief_candidates: readonly MemoryCandidate[];\n  readonly cost_units: number;\n  readonly metadata: Record<string, unknown>;\n}\n\ninterface FileUnit {\n  readonly path: string;\n  readonly relativePath: string;\n  readonly text: string;\n  readonly sourceFile: ts.SourceFile;\n  readonly isTest: boolean;\n}\n\ninterface SourceRisk {\n  readonly riskClass: string;\n  readonly highRisk: boolean;\n  readonly securitySensitive: boolean;\n  readonly migrationHazard: boolean;\n  readonly writeBoundary: boolean;\n}\n\ninterface AnalysisResult {\n  readonly observations: AdapterObservation[];\n  readonly findings: AdapterFinding[];\n  readonly readPaths: string[];\n}\n\ninterface PathAlias {\n  readonly prefix: string;\n  readonly suffix: string;\n  readonly targets: readonly string[];\n}\n\n\nexport function analyzeTestGaps(input: AdapterInput, workspaceRoot = process.cwd()): AriaOutput {\n  const roots = requireScanRoots('test-gap-adapter', input.roots);\n  const allowlist = new Set((input.allowlist ?? []).map(normalizePath));\n  const files = roots\n    .map((root) => resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot, root))\n    .filter((root) => workspacePathExists(root))\n    .flatMap((root) => collectSourceAndTestFiles(root));\n  const snapshotFiles = filterFilesBySnapshot(files, workspaceRoot, input);\n  const units = snapshotFiles.map((file) => readFileUnit(file, workspaceRoot));\n  const tests = units.filter((unit) => unit.isTest);\n  const sources = units.filter((unit) => !unit.isTest);\n  const sourcePaths = new Set(sources.map((source) => source.relativePath));\n  const pathAliases = readPathAliases(workspaceRoot);\n  const result: AnalysisResult = {\n    observations: [],\n    findings: [],\n    readPaths: units.map((unit) => unit.relativePath),\n  };\n\n  for (const test of tests) {\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `test-gap-test-file:${test.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'test_gap_test_file',\n      path: test.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      name: basename(test.relativePath),\n      details: {\n        imports: importedSpecifiers(test.sourceFile).sort(),\n      },\n    });\n  }\n\n  let highRiskWithoutTest = 0;\n  let directMatchCount = 0;\n  let weakMatchCount = 0;\n  for (const source of sources) {\n    const risk = classifySourceRisk(source);\n    if (!risk.highRisk && !risk.securitySensitive && !risk.migrationHazard) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    const matchedTests = matchingTests(source, tests, sourcePaths, pathAliases);\n    const weakMatchedTests = weakSymbolTests(source, tests).filter(\n      (test) => !matchedTests.some((matched) => matched.relativePath === test.relativePath),\n    );\n    directMatchCount += matchedTests.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;\n    weakMatchCount += weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 1 : 0;\n    const allowlisted = allowlist.has(source.relativePath);\n    result.observations.push({\n      id: `test-gap-source-file:${source.relativePath}`,\n      type: 'test_gap_source_file',\n      path: source.relativePath,\n      line: 1,\n      name: basename(source.relativePath),\n      details: {\n        riskClass: risk.riskClass,\n        highRisk: risk.highRisk,\n        securitySensitive: risk.securitySensitive,\n        migrationHazard: risk.migrationHazard,\n        writeBoundary: risk.writeBoundary,\n        matchedTests: matchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n        weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort(),\n        allowlisted,\n      },\n    });\n    if (allowlisted || matchedTests.length > 0) {\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (risk.migrationHazard) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `migration-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'migration_without_test',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'Hazardous migration has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.55 : 0.88,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (risk.securitySensitive) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `security-source-without-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'security_source_without_security_test',\n        severity: 'high',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'Security-sensitive source file has no adjacent or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.55 : 0.86,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (input.includeWriteBoundaryFindings === true && risk.highRisk && risk.writeBoundary) {\n      highRiskWithoutTest += 1;\n      result.findings.push({\n        id: `high-risk-source-without-adjacent-test:${source.relativePath}`,\n        rule: 'high_risk_source_without_adjacent_test',\n        severity: 'medium',\n        path: source.relativePath,\n        message: 'High-risk source file has no adjacent, sibling __tests__, or importing test coverage signal.',\n        evidence: [{ path: source.relativePath }],\n        confidence: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 0.5 : 0.75,\n        actionability: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'review_required' : 'actionable',\n        review_reason: weakMatchedTests.length > 0 ? 'weak_symbol_test_match' : undefined,\n        details: { riskClass: risk.riskClass, weakMatchedTests: weakMatchedTests.map((test) => test.relativePath).sort() },\n      });\n    }\n  }\n\n  result.observations.push({\n    id: 'test-gap-coverage-summary',\n    type: 'test_gap_coverage_summary',\n    details: {\n      sourceFiles: sources.length,\n      testFiles: tests.length,\n      directMatchCount,\n      weakMatchCount,\n      highRiskWithoutTest,\n      unmatchedHighRiskCount: highRiskWithoutTest,\n      scanMode: 'import_graph_v1',\n    },\n  });\n\n  result.observations.sort(compareById);\n  result.findings.sort(compareById);\n  result.readPaths.sort();\n  const evidenceSources = Array.from(\n    new Set(result.findings.flatMap((finding) => finding.evidence.map((evidence) => evidence.path))),\n  ).sort();\n\n  return {\n    observations: result.observations,\n    findings: result.findings,\n    read_paths: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).sort(),\n    evidence_sources: evidenceSources,\n    belief_candidates: [\n      {\n        belief_id: 'test-gap:high-risk-source-coverage-surface',\n        claim: 'high-risk backend, web security, and migration surfaces can be mapped to adjacent or importing test coverage signals',\n        confidence: 0.75,\n        evidence_refs: ['apps/**/*.ts', 'libs/**/*.ts', 'platform/libs/**/*.ts', 'web/**/*.tsx'],\n        source_tool_id: 'test-gap-adapter',\n      },\n    ],\n    cost_units: Array.from(new Set(result.readPaths)).length,\n    metadata: {\n      adapter: 'test-gap-adapter',\n      roots: roots.map(String).sort(),\n      files_scanned: snapshotFiles.length,\n      findings_count: result.findings.length,\n      allowlist_count: allowlist.size,\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction classifySourceRisk(unit: FileUnit): SourceRisk {\n  const path = unit.relativePath;\n  const text = unit.text;\n  const riskClass = riskClassForPath(path);\n  const highRisk = /(\\.controller|\\.resolver|\\.handler|\\.guard|\\.strategy|\\.middleware|\\.interceptor)\\.ts$/.test(path);\n  const securitySensitive = /dangerouslySetInnerHTML|DOMPurify|sanitizeHtml|@Public\\b|AuthGuard|TenantGuard|PermissionGuard/i.test(text)\n    || /(\\.guard|\\.strategy|\\/auth\\/|\\/security\\/|\\/permission)/i.test(path);\n  const writeBoundary = /@(Post|Put|Patch|Delete|Mutation)\\b|\\b(create|update|delete|remove|archive|approve|reject|reset|invite)\\w*\\s*\\(/i.test(text);\n  const migrationHazard = /\\/migrations\\/.*\\.ts$/.test(path)\n    && /\\b(DROP|DELETE|TRUNCATE|ALTER\\s+TYPE|ENABLE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY|FORCE\\s+ROW\\s+LEVEL\\s+SECURITY)\\b/i.test(text);\n  return { riskClass, highRisk, securitySensitive, migrationHazard, writeBoundary };\n}\n\nfunction riskClassForPath(path: string): string {\n  if (path.includes('/migrations/')) {\n    return 'migration';\n  }\n  // match[1] is narrowed explicitly so the file stays clean under\n  // --noUncheckedIndexedAccess (the capture group is structurally guaranteed,\n  // but the index signature cannot prove it to the compiler).\n  const match = path.match(/\\.([a-z-]+)\\.(ts|tsx)$/);\n  if (match?.[1] !== undefined) {\n    return match[1];\n  }\n  if (path.endsWith('.tsx')) {\n    return 'ui_component';\n  }\n  return 'source';\n}\n\nfunction matchingTests(\n  source: FileUnit,\n  tests: readonly FileUnit[],\n  sourcePaths: ReadonlySet<string>,\n  pathAliases: readonly PathAlias[],\n): readonly FileUnit[] {\n  const sourceBase = basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(source.relativePath);\n  const sourceDir = dirname(source.relativePath);\n  return tests.filter((test) => {\n    const testBase = basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(test.relativePath);\n    if (testBase === sourceBase) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    if (dirname(test.relativePath) === sourceDir && testBase.startsWith(sourceBase)) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    if (dirname(test.relativePath).endsWith(`${sourceDir}/__tests__`) && testBase.includes(sourceBase)) {\n      return true;\n    }\n    return importedSpecifiers(test.sourceFile).some((specifier) => {\n      const resolved = resolveImportSpecifier(test.relativePath, specifier, sourcePaths, pathAliases);\n      return resolved === source.relativePath;\n    });\n  });\n}\n\nfunction weakSymbolTests(source: FileUnit, tests: readonly FileUnit[]): readonly FileUnit[] {\n  const symbolNames = exportedSymbols(source.sourceFile);\n  if (symbolNames.length === 0) {\n    return [];\n  }\n  return tests.filter((test) => symbolNames.some((symbolName) => symbolName.length > 2 && test.text.includes(symbolName)));\n}\n\nfunction basenameWithoutKnownSuffix(path: string): string {\n  return basename(path)\n    .replace(/\\.(spec|test)\\.(ts|tsx)$/, '')\n    .replace(/\\.(controller|resolver|handler|guard|strategy|middleware|interceptor|service|entity|module|component)\\.(ts|tsx)$/, '')\n    .replace(/\\.(ts|tsx)$/, '');\n}\n\nfunction importedSpecifiers(sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string[] {\n  const imports: string[] = [];\n  for (const statement of sourceFile.statements) {\n    if (ts.isImportDeclaration(statement) && ts.isStringLiteral(statement.moduleSpecifier)) {\n      imports.push(statement.moduleSpecifier.text);\n    }\n  }\n  return imports;\n}\n\nfunction resolveImportSpecifier(\n  importingPath: string,\n  specifier: string,\n  sourcePaths: ReadonlySet<string>,\n  pathAliases: readonly PathAlias[],\n): string | undefined {\n  const candidates: string[] = [];\n  if (specifier.startsWith('.')) {\n    candidates.push(normalizePath(join(dirname(importingPath), specifier)));\n  } else {\n    for (const alias of pathAliases) {\n      if (!specifier.startsWith(alias.prefix) || !specifier.endsWith(alias.suffix)) {\n        continue;\n      }\n      const middle = specifier.slice(alias.prefix.length, specifier.length - alias.suffix.length);\n      for (const target of alias.targets) {\n        candidates.push(target.replace('*', middle));\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  for (const candidate of candidates.flatMap(candidatePathVariants)) {\n    if (sourcePaths.has(candidate)) {\n      return candidate;\n    }\n  }\n  return undefined;\n}\n\nfunction candidatePathVariants(candidate: string): string[] {\n  const normalized = normalizePath(candidate).replace(/^\\.\\//, '');\n  return [\n    normalized,\n    `${normalized}.ts`,\n    `${normalized}.tsx`,\n    `${normalized}/index.ts`,\n    `${normalized}/index.tsx`,\n  ];\n}\n\nfunction readPathAliases(workspaceRoot: string): readonly PathAlias[] {\n  const path = resolve(workspaceRoot, 'tsconfig.base.json');\n  if (!workspacePathExists(path)) {\n    return [];\n  }\n  try {\n    const parsed = JSON.parse(readWorkspaceFile(path)) as {\n      compilerOptions?: { paths?: Record<string, readonly string[]> };\n    };\n    return Object.entries(parsed.compilerOptions?.paths ?? {}).map(([key, targets]) => {\n      const starIndex = key.indexOf('*');\n      return {\n        prefix: starIndex >= 0 ? key.slice(0, starIndex) : key,\n        suffix: starIndex >= 0 ? key.slice(starIndex + 1) : '',\n        targets: targets.map((target) => normalizePath(target)),\n      };\n    });\n  } catch {\n    return [];\n  }\n}\n\nfunction exportedSymbols(sourceFile: ts.SourceFile): string[] {\n  const symbols = new Set<string>();\n  visit(sourceFile, (node) => {\n    if ((ts.isClassDeclaration(node) || ts.isFunctionDeclaration(node) || ts.isInterfaceDeclaration(node) || ts.isTypeAliasDeclaration(node)) && node.name) {\n      const modifiers = ts.canHaveModifiers(node) ? ts.getModifiers(node) ?? [] : [];\n      if (modifiers.some((modifier) => modifier.kind === ts.SyntaxKind.ExportKeyword)) {\n        symbols.add(node.name.text);\n      }\n    }\n  });\n  return Array.from(symbols).sort();\n}\n\nfunction readFileUnit(path: string, workspaceRoot: string): FileUnit {\n  const text = readWorkspaceFile(path);\n  return {\n    path,\n    relativePath: normalizePath(relative(workspaceRoot, path)),\n    text,\n    sourceFile: ts.createSourceFile(path, text, ts.ScriptTarget.Latest, true),\n    isTest: isTestFile(path),\n  };\n}\n\nfunction collectSourceAndTestFiles(root: string): readonly string[] {\n  return collectFiles(root, {\n    extensions: ['.ts', '.tsx'],\n    // E13 FP class (2): archived corpus (e.g. migrations/.archive/<timestamp>/)\n    // is dead code — it can neither need test coverage nor PROVIDE it (an\n    // archived spec must not satisfy a live source), so it is excluded from\n    // the scan entirely rather than special-cased at finding time.\n    includeFile: (name, path) => !name.endsWith('.d.ts') && !isArchivedWorkspacePath(path),\n  });\n}\n\nfunction isTestFile(path: string): boolean {\n  const normalized = normalizePath(path);\n  return /(__tests__|\\.spec\\.|\\.test\\.)/.test(normalized);\n}\n\nfunction resolveInsideWorkspace(workspaceRoot: string, requestedPath: string): string {\n  return resolveAdapterPath(workspaceRoot, requestedPath);\n}\n\nfunction visit(node: ts.Node, visitor: (node: ts.Node) => void): void {\n  visitor(node);\n  ts.forEachChild(node, (child) => visit(child, visitor));\n}\n\nfunction compareById(a: { readonly id: string }, b: { readonly id: string }): number {\n  return a.id.localeCompare(b.id);\n}\n\nfunction normalizePath(path: string): string {\n  return normalizeWorkspacePath(path);\n}\n\nfunction readStdin(): Promise<string> {\n  return new Promise((resolvePromise, reject) => {\n    let input = '';\n    process.stdin.setEncoding('utf8');\n    // setEncoding('utf8') makes every chunk a string at runtime, but the\n    // stream's declared chunk type stays `string | Buffer` — concatenating the\n    // union is what the type checker rejects. Narrow at the boundary rather\n    // than widening `input` (kernel-dead-wire-adapter is the converged shape).\n    process.stdin.on('data', (chunk: string | Buffer) => {\n      input += typeof chunk === 'string' ? chunk : chunk.toString('utf8');\n    });\n    process.stdin.on('end', () => resolvePromise(input));\n    process.stdin.on('error', reject);\n  });\n}\n\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n  const rawInput = await readStdin();\n  const input = rawInput.trim().length > 0 ? (JSON.parse(rawInput) as AdapterInput) : {};\n  process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(analyzeTestGaps(input))}\\n`);\n}\n\nif (require.main === module) {\n  main().catch((error: unknown) => {\n    const message = error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);\n    process.stderr.write(`${message}\\n`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  });\n}\n","numLines":494,"startLine":1,"totalLines":494}}}

Returns 'unknown' if the package is\n * not resolvable (e.g. in a bundled/stripped deployment).\n */\nfunction readPackageVersion(packageJsonPath: string): string {\n  try {\n    // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-var-requires\n    return require(packageJsonPath).version;\n  } catch {\n    return 'unknown';\n  }\n}\n\n/**\n * Runtime framework versions exposed by the health endpoint.\n * Used to identify NestJS v10 vs v11 and Express v4 vs v5 during\n * phased upgrade rollouts (ADR-013 Section 8.4).\n */\nexport interface FrameworkVersionInfo {\n  /** NestJS framework version — e.g. \"10.4.15\" or \"11.0.1\" */\n  nestjs: string;\n  /** Express HTTP adapter version — v4 (NestJS v10) vs v5 (NestJS v11) */\n  express: string;\n  /** Node.js runtime version */\n  node: string;\n}\n\n/**\n * Standard health check response for the general /health endpoint.\n * Contains only non-sensitive information safe for public exposure.\n */\nexport interface StandardHealthResponse {\n  status: 'ok';\n  timestamp: string;\n  uptime: number;\n  version: string;\n  /** Service name for identification in multi-service monitoring */\n  service: string;\n  /** Framework versions for v10/v11 identification during phased upgrade */\n  framework: FrameworkVersionInfo;\n}\n\n/**\n * Readiness check response for the /health/ready endpoint.\n * Reports service readiness and dependency checks.\n */\nexport interface ReadinessResponse {\n  status: 'ok' | 'degraded' | 'not_ready';\n  checks: {\n    database: 'ok' | 'error';\n    [key: string]: 'ok' | 'error';\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * Configuration options for the StandardHealthController.\n * Services can extend this controller and provide service-specific options.\n */\nexport interface HealthControllerOptions {\n  /**\n   * Service name for logging purposes.\n   */\n  serviceName: string;\n\n  /**\n   * Service version. Defaults to process.env.APP_VERSION or '0.0.0'.\n   */\n  version?: string;\n\n  /**\n   * Additional readiness checks beyond the standard database check.\n   * Each check returns 'ok' or 'error'.\n   */\n  additionalChecks?: () => Promise<Record<string, 'ok' | 'error'>>;\n}\n\n/**\n * StandardHealthController\n *\n * Provides a unified health check format across all microservices for K8s probes:\n *\n *   GET /health/live  -> Liveness probe. Always returns 200 if process is alive.\n *   GET /health/ready -> Readiness probe. Returns 200 if database (and custom checks)\n *                        are reachable, 503 otherwise.\n *   GET /health       -> General health. Returns sanitized status with timestamp,\n *                        uptime, and version. No sensitive data.\n *\n * All endpoints are public (no auth/tenant required) and skip throttling so that\n * K8s probes and load balancers always get a response.\n *\n * Services can extend this controller to add service-specific checks (e.g. TimescaleDB,\n * NATS, MQTT) by overriding `getAdditionalChecks()`.\n */\n@Controller('health')\n@Public()\n@SkipThrottle()\nexport class StandardHealthController {\n  protected serviceName = 'service';\n  protected version: string = process.env['APP_VERSION'] || '0.0.0';\n\n  private _logger?: Logger;\n\n  /** Lazily creates a Logger using the (possibly overridden) serviceName. */\n  protected get logger(): Logger {\n    if (!this._logger) {\n      this._logger = new Logger(`${this.serviceName}:HealthController`);\n    }\n    return this._logger;\n  }\n\n  /** Allow subclasses to replace the logger. */\n  protected set logger(value: Logger) {\n    this._logger = value;\n  }\n\n  constructor(\n    @InjectDataSource()\n    protected readonly dataSource: DataSource,\n  ) {}\n\n  /**\n   * K8s Liveness Probe.\n   * Returns 200 as long as the process is running.\n   * K8s restarts the pod if this fails.\n   */\n  @Get('live')\n  @HttpCode(HttpStatus.OK)\n  liveness(): { status: 'ok' } {\n    return { status: 'ok' };\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * K8s Readiness Probe.\n   * Returns 200 if the database is reachable (via `SELECT 1`).\n   * Returns 503 if the database is unreachable.\n   * K8s removes the pod from service endpoints if this fails.\n   */\n  @Get('ready')\n  async readiness(@Res() res: Response): Promise<void> {\n    const checks: Record<string, 'ok' | 'error'> = {\n      database: await this.checkDatabase(),\n    };\n\n    // Merge additional checks from subclasses\n    const extra = await this.getAdditionalChecks();\n    Object.assign(checks, extra);\n\n    const hasError = Object.values(checks).some((v) => v === 'error');\n    const allError = Object.values(checks).every((v) => v === 'error');\n\n    let status: ReadinessResponse['status'];\n    if (allError) {\n      status = 'not_ready';\n    } else if (hasError) {\n      status = 'degraded';\n    } else {\n      status = 'ok';\n    }\n\n    const httpStatus =\n      status === 'not_ready'\n        ? HttpStatus.SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE\n        : HttpStatus.OK;\n\n    const body: ReadinessResponse = { status, checks: checks as ReadinessResponse['checks'] };\n    res.status(httpStatus).json(body);\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * General Health Endpoint.\n   * Returns sanitized health information (no memory, connection counts, etc.).\n   * Safe for public exposure.\n   *\n   * ADR-013 Section 8.4: Includes framework version info so operators can\n   * identify whether a running service is NestJS v10 or v11 during phased\n   * upgrade rollouts. The `framework` block exposes NestJS core version,\n   * Express adapter version, and Node.js runtime version.\n   */\n  @Get()\n  @HttpCode(HttpStatus.OK)\n  health(): StandardHealthResponse {\n    return {\n      status: 'ok',\n      timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),\n      uptime: process.uptime(),\n      version: this.version,\n      service: this.serviceName,\n      framework: {\n        nestjs: readPackageVersion('@nestjs/core/package.json'),\n        express: readPackageVersion('express/package.json'),\n        node: process.version,\n      },\n    };\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Check database connectivity using a real query.\n   * Subclasses should NOT override this; use getAdditionalChecks() instead.\n   */\n  protected async checkDatabase(): Promise<'ok' | 'error'> {\n    try {\n      if (!this.dataSource.isInitialized) {\n        return 'error';\n      }\n      await this.dataSource.query('SELECT 1');\n      return 'ok';\n    } catch (error) {\n      this.logger.warn(\n        `Database health check failed: ${(error as Error).message}`,\n      );\n      return 'error';\n    }\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Override in subclasses to add service-specific readiness checks.\n   * Return a map of check-name -> 'ok' | 'error'.\n   *\n   * @example\n   * // In SensorHealthController:\n   * protected async getAdditionalChecks() {\n   *   return { timescale: await this.checkTimescale() };\n   * }\n   */\n  protected async getAdditionalChecks(): Promise<Record<string, 'ok' | 'error'>> {\n    return {};\n  }\n}\n","numLines":246,"startLine":1,"totalLines":246}}}

* Farm Service Health Controller Unit Tests\n *\n * Tests the standardized health check format:\n *   GET /health/live  -> { status: 'ok' }\n *   GET /health/ready -> { status, checks: { database } }\n *   GET /health       -> { status, timestamp, uptime, version }\n */\n\nimport { Test, TestingModule } from '@nestjs/testing';\nimport { DataSource } from 'typeorm';\nimport { HealthController } from '../health.controller';\nimport { TenantSchemaReadinessService } from '../tenant-schema-readiness.service';\n\n// Mock response object for @Res() endpoints\nconst createMockResponse = () => {\n  const res: any = {\n    status: jest.fn().mockReturnThis(),\n    json: jest.fn().mockReturnThis(),\n  };\n  return res;\n};\n\ndescribe('HealthController (Farm Service)', () => {\n  let controller: HealthController;\n  let queryMock: jest.Mock;\n  let isInitialized: boolean;\n  // The tenant-schema readiness slice is its own service with its own unit\n  // spec (tenant-schema-readiness.service.spec.ts). Here we mock it so these\n  // tests stay focused on the controller's standard probe behaviour; default\n  // it to 'ok' so existing readiness assertions are unaffected.\n  let tenantSchemaCheck: jest.Mock;\n\n  const createMockDataSource = () => ({\n    get isInitialized() {\n      return isInitialized;\n    },\n    query: queryMock,\n  });\n\n  beforeEach(async () => {\n    isInitialized = true;\n    queryMock = jest.fn().mockResolvedValue([{ '?column?': 1 }]);\n    tenantSchemaCheck = jest.fn().mockResolvedValue('ok');\n\n    const module: TestingModule = await Test.createTestingModule({\n      controllers: [HealthController],\n      providers: [\n        {\n          provide: DataSource,\n          useFactory: createMockDataSource,\n        },\n        {\n          provide: TenantSchemaReadinessService,\n          useValue: { checkTenantSchemaRouting: tenantSchemaCheck },\n        },\n      ],\n    }).compile();\n\n    controller = module.get<HealthController>(HealthController);\n  });\n\n  describe('liveness', () => {\n    it('should return ok status', () => {\n      const result = controller.liveness();\n      expect(result).toEqual({ status: 'ok' });\n    });\n\n    it('should always succeed regardless of database state', () => {\n      isInitialized = false;\n      const result = controller.liveness();\n      expect(result).toEqual({ status: 'ok' });\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('readiness', () => {\n    it('should return ok when database is connected', async () => {\n      isInitialized = true;\n      queryMock.mockResolvedValue([{ '?column?': 1 }]);\n      const res = createMockResponse();\n\n      await controller.readiness(res);\n\n      expect(res.status).toHaveBeenCalledWith(200);\n      expect(res.json).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n        status: 'ok',\n        checks: { database: 'ok', tenant_schema: 'ok' },\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('should return 503 when database is not connected', async () => {\n      isInitialized = false;\n      // When the DB is unreachable the tenant-schema slice also fails closed.\n      tenantSchemaCheck.mockResolvedValue('error');\n      const res = createMockResponse();\n\n      await controller.readiness(res);\n\n      expect(res.status).toHaveBeenCalledWith(503);\n      expect(res.json).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n        status: 'not_ready',\n        checks: { database: 'error', tenant_schema: 'error' },\n      });\n    });\n\n    it('should report degraded (200) when tenant-schema routing is broken but DB is ok', async () => {\n      isInitialized = true;\n      tenantSchemaCheck.mockResolvedValue('error');\n      const res = createMockResponse();\n\n      await controller.readiness(res);\n\n      // database ok + tenant_schema error => degraded, still 200 per the\n      // StandardHealthController aggregation rules.\n      expect(res.status).toHaveBeenCalledWith(200);\n      expect(res.json).toHaveBeenCalledWith({\n        status: 'degraded',\n        checks: { database: 'ok', tenant_schema: 'error' },\n      });\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('health', () => {\n    it('should return standardized health status', () => {\n      const result = controller.health();\n\n      expect(result.status).toBe('ok');\n      expect(result.timestamp).toBeDefined();\n      expect(typeof result.uptime).toBe('number');\n      expect(result.uptime).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0);\n      expect(result.version).toBeDefined();\n    });\n\n    it('should include valid ISO timestamp', () => {\n      const result = controller.health();\n      const timestamp = new Date(result.timestamp);\n      expect(timestamp.toISOString()).toBe(result.timestamp);\n    });\n\n    it('should report actual process uptime', () => {\n      const beforeUptime = process.uptime();\n      const result = controller.health();\n      const afterUptime = process.uptime();\n\n      expect(result.uptime).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(beforeUptime);\n      expect(result.uptime).toBeLessThanOrEqual(afterUptime);\n    });\n  });\n\n  describe('Controller Decorators', () => {\n    it('should be decorated with @Controller(\"health\")', () => {\n      const controllerPath = Reflect.getMetadata('path', HealthController);\n      expect(controllerPath).toBe('health');\n    });\n  });\n});\n","numLines":157,"startLine":1,"totalLines":157}}}

Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n506\t    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n507\t    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n508\t    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n509\t    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n510\t    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n511\t    try:\n512\t        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n513\t    except _GE as exc:\n514\t        msg = str(exc)\n515\t        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n516\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n517\t        else:\n518\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n519\t                           \"error\": msg})\n520\t        return\n521\t    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n522\t    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n523\t    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n524\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n525\t        return\n526\t    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n527\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n528\t        return\n529\t    checked.append(rel_str)\n530\t    if line is None:\n531\t        return\n532\t    if line <= 0:\n533\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n534\t        return\n535\t    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n536\t    if line > line_count:\n537\t        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n538\t\n539\t\n540\tdef validate_agent_response_evidence(\n541\t    *,\n542\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n543\t    workspace_root: str | Path,\n544\t    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n545\t    probe_session: GitProbeSession | None = None,\n546\t) -> dict[str, Any]:\n547\t    \"\"\"Re-fetch every evidence ref claimed by an agent response and verify it exists in the repo.\n548\t\n549\t    Why: Plan 016 V-24 — agent output is data, not truth. The contract\n550\t    layer (agent_contract.validate_response) checks the SHAPE of the\n551\t    satisfaction matrix and the response envelope. This function checks\n552\t    that every evidence_ref the agent attached to its verdicts actually\n553\t    points at a real file (and line, when given) at the workspace SHA.\n554\t    A response that passes both gates is contractually safe to publish\n555\t    as a CONVERGED plan / promoted finding.\n556\t\n557\t    Returns: {\n558\t        \"valid\": bool,"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f71484da-9cca-4504-a5c2-f8e1c65217d4","uuid":"71624efa-5be7-4bdc-8f00-b0549b9a5164","timestamp":"2026-09-19T14:33:10.119Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-e340d37a0d97/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/evidence_validator.py","content":"    parsed = _parse_agent_ref(ref)\n    if parsed is None:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_ref_malformed\", \"ref\": ref})\n        return\n    path, line = parsed\n    # Plan 024 v3 §H-5 — canonical-resolve BEFORE the SELF_OUTPUT\n    # prefix check. Pre-fix the prefix match operated on the\n    # lexically-normalized string; a `src/../aria-tools/...` traversal\n    # could bypass detection if normalize_path didn't fully collapse\n    # it. Post-fix the resolution runs first, then SELF_OUTPUT is\n    # decided on the canonical posix-relative form.\n    from .tool_registry import GovernanceError as _GE\n    try:\n        rel_str, absolute = _canonical_evidence_path(path, root)\n    except _GE as exc:\n        msg = str(exc)\n        if msg.startswith(\"evidence_path_outside_repo\"):\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_escapes_workspace\", \"path\": path})\n        else:\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_unresolvable\", \"path\": path,\n                           \"error\": msg})\n        return\n    # ARIA self-output / runtime artefacts are NOT admissible evidence\n    # for an agent claim (CLAUDE.md L1, Plan 016 §Agent output untrusted).\n    if not allow_self_output and rel_str.startswith(SELF_OUTPUT_MARKERS):\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_self_output\", \"path\": rel_str})\n        return\n    if not absolute.exists() or not absolute.is_file():\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_path_missing\", \"path\": path})\n        return\n    checked.append(rel_str)\n    if line is None:\n        return\n    if line <= 0:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_invalid\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line})\n        return\n    line_count = len(absolute.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines())\n    if line > line_count:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_evidence_line_missing\", \"path\": path, \"line\": line, \"line_count\": line_count})\n\n\ndef validate_agent_response_evidence(\n    *,\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    workspace_root: str | Path,\n    request: dict[str, Any] | None = None,\n    probe_session: GitProbeSession | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Re-fetch every evidence ref claimed by an agent response and verify it exists in the repo.\n\n    Why: Plan 016 V-24 — agent output is data, not truth. The contract\n    layer (agent_contract.validate_response) checks the SHAPE of the\n    satisfaction matrix and the response envelope. This function checks\n    that every evidence_ref the agent attached to its verdicts actually\n    points at a real file (and line, when given) at the workspace SHA.\n    A response that passes both gates is contractually safe to publish\n    as a CONVERGED plan / promoted finding.\n\n    Returns: {\n        \"valid\": bool,","numLines":60,"startLine":499,"totalLines":774}}}

present.\n683\t    if request is not None:\n684\t        if \"allowed_scope\" not in request:\n685\t            errors.append({\"code\": \"evidence_request_missing_allowed_scope\"})\n686\t        else:\n687\t            allowed_globs = list(request.get(\"allowed_scope\") or [])\n688\t            allowed_request_refs = {\n689\t                (_parse_agent_ref(r) or (\"\", None))[0]\n690\t                for r in (request.get(\"evidence_refs\") or [])\n691\t                if isinstance(r, str)\n692\t            }\n693\t            for path in checked:\n694\t                if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(path):\n695\t                    continue  # The\n    # request can opt out via `allow_empty_satisfaction_matrix=True`\n    # for the rare judge-only domain that explicitly does not produce\n    # per-criterion evidence — operator-approval-gated; not a default.\n    matrix = response.get(\"satisfaction_matrix\") or []\n    allow_empty_matrix = bool(request and request.get(\"allow_empty_satisfaction_matrix\"))\n    if not matrix and not allow_empty_matrix:\n        errors.append({\"code\": \"evidence_satisfaction_matrix_must_be_non_empty\"})\n    if isinstance(matrix, list):\n        for entry in matrix:\n            if not isinstance(entry, dict):\n                continue\n            entry_refs = entry.get(\"evidence_refs\") or []\n            if not isinstance(entry_refs, list):\n                errors.append({\"code\": \"agent_matrix_evidence_refs_not_list\", \"id\": entry.get(\"id\")})\n                continue\n            for ref in entry_refs:\n                if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref.strip():\n                    errors.append(\n                        {\"code\": \"agent_matrix_evidence_ref_not_string\", \"id\": entry.get(\"id\")}\n                    )\n                    continue\n                _check_agent_ref(\n                    ref, root=root, errors=errors, checked=checked,\n                    allow_kernel_artifacts=allow_kernel_artifacts,\n                )\n                if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(ref):\n                    continue  # Pre-fix the empty-list case skipped scope enforcement\n    # entirely under \"preserves backward compatibility with legacy\n    # aria/agent-invocation-request/v1 rows that pre-date Plan 016\".\n    # That path is now closed: legacy rows are caught at\n    # _strict_request_view (line ~964); by the time validate_agent_-\n    # response_evidence sees the request the strict fields are\n    # present.\n    if request is not None:\n        if \"allowed_scope\" not in request:\n            errors.append({\"code\": \"evidence_request_missing_allowed_scope\"})\n        else:\n            allowed_globs = list(request.get(\"allowed_scope\") or [])\n            allowed_request_refs = {\n                (_parse_agent_ref(r) or (\"\", None))[0]\n                for r in (request.get(\"evidence_refs\") or [])\n                if isinstance(r, str)\n            }\n            for path in checked:\n                if _is_ledger_pointer_ref(path):\n                    continue  # Z2 — pointer identity is bound at mint, not by glob\n                if allow_kernel_artifacts and _kernel_artifact_verdict(path, root)[0]:\n                    # ORPHAN-734 — an arbitrated artifact's identity is bound\n                    # by the results ledger's content hash, not by a repo glob\n                    # (the same reason the pointer above skips this check).\n                    continue\n                if path in allowed_request_refs:\n                    continue\n                if not _path_matches_any_glob(path, allowed_globs):\n                    errors.append(\n                        {\n                            \"code\": \"agent_evidence_outside_allowed_scope\",\n                            \"path\": path,\n                            \"allowed_scope\": allowed_globs,\n                        }\n                    )\n\n    return {\n        \"valid\": not errors,\n        \"errors\": errors,\n        \"checked_refs\": sorted(set(checked)),\n        \"evidence_envelopes\": evidence_envelopes,\n    }\n\n","numLines":105,"startLine":615,"totalLines":774}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01P5yVvwojdAs35hG1hqxemz","type":"tool_result","content":"60\t        return \"low\"\n61\t    return fallback\n62\t\n63\t\n64\tdef _verdict_field(details: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n65\t    \"\"\"The judge .md prompts shape verdicts under details.verdict (evidence,\n66\t    adversarial) or details.consensus (arbiter). Return whichever exists.\n67\t    \"\"\"\n68\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n69\t        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n70\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n71\t        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n72\t    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n73\t        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n74\t        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n75\t        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n76\t        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n77\t        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n78\t        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n79\t        # stays the only authority on validity.\n80\t        return {\n81\t            key: details.get(key)\n82\t            for key in (\n83\t                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n84\t                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n85\t                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n86\t            )\n87\t            if details.get(key) is not None\n88\t        }\n89\t    return {}\n90\t\n91\t\n92\tdef validate_judge_response(\n93\t    *,\n94\t    request: dict[str, Any],\n95\t    response: dict[str, Any],\n96\t) -> list[str]:\n97\t    \"\"\"Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — the judge output contract, checkable BEFORE accept.\n98\t\n99\t    The second sealed night accepted 12 judge results whose envelopes never\n100\t    carried a verdict the bridge could read (\"expected verdict ..., got\n101\t    None\") — each one an accepted row that could never fold, burning bridge\n102\t    retries toward permanent_fail. This is the SINGLE definition of what a\n103\t    judge response must carry; ``record_judge_verdict_from_response`` raises\n104\t    on exactly this list, and the executor's pre-submit gate releases the\n105\t    claim on it instead of sealing an unfoldable result.\n106\t\n107\t    Returns [] for non-judge roles: the contract binds judges only.\n108\t    \"\"\"\n109\t    role = response.get(\"role\")\n110\t    if not is_judge_role(role):\n111\t        return []\n112\t    errors: list[str] = []\n113\t    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n114\t    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n115\t        details = {}\n116\t    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n117\t    if not verdict_block:\n118\t        return [\"judge_verdict:absent\"]\n119\t    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f71484da-9cca-4504-a5c2-f8e1c65217d4","uuid":"0186812c-fb67-4dc9-a372-b6ddbd49b214","timestamp":"2026-09-19T14:34:24.469Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-e340d37a0d97/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/judgment_bridge.py","content":"        return \"low\"\n    return fallback\n\n\ndef _verdict_field(details: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"The judge .md prompts shape verdicts under details.verdict (evidence,\n    adversarial) or details.consensus (arbiter). Return whichever exists.\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"verdict\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"consensus\"), dict):\n        return dict(details[\"consensus\"])\n    if isinstance(details.get(\"verdict\"), str):\n        # Flat shape (ORPHAN-HIGH-629 sibling): the judge contract file says\n        # BOTH \"Return JSON with: verdict, judge_id, confidence...\" and\n        # \"details.verdict.rationale\". The first live judge run answered\n        # flat, was ACCEPTED by the envelope gate, and dropped HERE with\n        # \"expected verdict ..., got None\" — a verdict lost between two\n        # halves of one document. Honour both spellings; the envelope gate\n        # stays the only authority on validity.\n        return {\n            key: details.get(key)\n            for key in (\n                \"verdict\", \"tool_id\", \"run_id\", \"finding_id\", \"judge_id\",\n                \"model\", \"prompt_hash\", \"confidence\", \"rationale\",\n                \"evidence_refs\", \"judgment_group_id\", \"severity\",\n            )\n            if details.get(key) is not None\n        }\n    return {}\n\n\ndef validate_judge_response(\n    *,\n    request: dict[str, Any],\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n) -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — the judge output contract, checkable BEFORE accept.\n\n    The second sealed night accepted 12 judge results whose envelopes never\n    carried a verdict the bridge could read (\"expected verdict ..., got\n    None\") — each one an accepted row that could never fold, burning bridge\n    retries toward permanent_fail. This is the SINGLE definition of what a\n    judge response must carry; ``record_judge_verdict_from_response`` raises\n    on exactly this list, and the executor's pre-submit gate releases the\n    claim on it instead of sealing an unfoldable result.\n\n    Returns [] for non-judge roles: the contract binds judges only.\n    \"\"\"\n    role = response.get(\"role\")\n    if not is_judge_role(role):\n        return []\n    errors: list[str] = []\n    details = response.get(\"details\") or {}\n    if not isinstance(details, dict):\n        details = {}\n    verdict_block = _verdict_field(details)\n    if not verdict_block:\n        return [\"judge_verdict:absent\"]\n    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")","numLines":60,"startLine":60,"totalLines":388}}}

verdict + severity from\n165\t    the response.details).\n166\t    \"\"\"\n167\t    role = response.get(\"role\")\n168\t    if not is_judge_role(role):\n169\t        return None\n170\t\n171\t    # Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — one contract, two consumers: the executor's\n172\t    # pre-submit gate checks exactly this list and releases instead of\n173\t    # sealing an unfoldable accepted result; the bridge raises on it so a\n174\t    # row that somehow slipped past still fails loudly, never half-folds.\n175\t    contract_errors = validate_judge_response(request=request, response=response)\n176\t    if contract_errors:\n177\t        raise GovernanceError(\n178\t            \"judge bridge contract violation: \" + \"; \".join(contract_errors)"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"f71484da-9cca-4504-a5c2-f8e1c65217d4","uuid":"9d30d3e7-76f7-4943-b9c5-eadb9440c9ea","timestamp":"2026-09-19T14:34:31.522Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/home/gharunner/actions-runner/_work/aquaculture_platform/aquaculture_platform/aria-worktrees/req-AIR-aria-evidence-judge-e340d37a0d97/aria-kernel/aria_kernel/judgment_bridge.py","content":"    verdict = verdict_block.get(\"verdict\")\n    if verdict not in FEEDBACK_VERDICTS:\n        # ORPHAN-CRITICAL-735 — the arbiter's contract says \"return\n        # details.consensus, OR the uncertainty reason when the consensus\n        # gate cannot be met\", and the deterministic engine it mirrors\n        # emits exactly that non-verdict outcome. The live arbiter obeyed\n        # and this contract burned the claim as invalid:None — fourth\n        # mint-vs-law instance. Uncertainty is a VALID arbitration\n        # outcome, for the arbiter alone, with a reason from the closed\n        # vocabulary; binary judges stay bound to binary verdicts.\n        reason = verdict_block.get(\"uncertainty_reason\")\n        if (\n            role == \"consensus_arbitration\"\n            and verdict is None\n            and reason in CONSENSUS_UNCERTAINTY_REASONS\n        ):\n            pass\n        else:\n            errors.append(f\"judge_verdict.verdict:invalid:{verdict!r}\")\n    tool_id = request.get(\"tool_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"tool_id\")\n    run_id = request.get(\"run_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"run_id\")\n    finding_id = request.get(\"finding_id\") or verdict_block.get(\"finding_id\")\n    if not (tool_id and run_id and finding_id):\n        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:missing_tool_run_or_finding_id\")\n    judge_id = details.get(\"agent_subagent_type\") or verdict_block.get(\"judge_id\")\n    if not judge_id:\n        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:missing_judge_identity\")\n    elif str(judge_id).startswith(\"ci-executor:\"):\n        errors.append(\"judge_verdict:executor_shaped_judge_identity\")\n    return errors\n\n\ndef record_judge_verdict_from_response(\n    *,\n    request: dict[str, Any],\n    response: dict[str, Any],\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any] | None:\n    \"\"\"Translate an ACCEPTED judge response into an ai_judge feedback row.\n\n    Returns the persisted row, or None when the envelope role is not a\n    judge role (the bridge is a no-op for non-judge submissions).\n\n    Raises GovernanceError when the response is missing the data the\n    consensus engine requires (tool_id / run_id / finding_id come from\n    the request envelope's optional fields; verdict + severity from\n    the response.details).\n    \"\"\"\n    role = response.get(\"role\")\n    if not is_judge_role(role):\n        return None\n\n    # Y5 (ORPHAN-706) — one contract, two consumers: the executor's\n    # pre-submit gate checks exactly this list and releases instead of\n    # sealing an unfoldable accepted result; the bridge raises on it so a\n    # row that somehow slipped past still fails loudly, never half-folds.\n    contract_errors = validate_judge_response(request=request, response=response)\n    if contract_errors:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            \"judge bridge contract violation: \" + \"; \".join(contract_errors)","numLines":60,"startLine":119,"totalLines":388}}}

   if payload is None:\n        raise GovernanceError(\n            f\"supporting bridge expects response.details.{payload_key} for role {role!r}\"\n        )\n\n    root = ensure_tools_dir(base_dir)\n    out_dir = root / \"judgment-pipeline\" / role\n    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    out_path = out_dir / f\"{request_id}.json\"\n    out_path.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2, sort_keys=True), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n    append_tools_governance(\n        root,\n        f\"agent_supporting_payload_persisted\",\n        {\n            \"role\": role,\n            \"request_id\": request_id,\n            \"path\": out_path.relative_to(root).as_posix(),\n            \"at\": utc_now(),\n        },\n    )\n    return {\"role\": role, \"request_id\": request_id, \"path\": str(out_path)}\n\n\ndef run_consensus(\n    *,\n    tool_id: str,\n    cycle_id: str | None = None,\n    min_confidence: float = CONSENSUS_MIN_CONFIDENCE,\n    base_dir: str | Path | None = None,\n) -> dict[str, Any]:\n    \"\"\"Thin wrapper over feedback_store.generate_ai_consensus.\n\n    Why a wrapper: Plan 016 wants the consensus invocation to be reachable\n    via a stable kernel CLI (`aria-kernel consensus run`) without\n    callers having to import feedback_store directly. The wrapper also\n    emits a governance event so a consensus run lands in the same\n    audit trail as the lease lifecycle events.\n    \"\"\"\n    result = generate_ai_consensus(\n        tool_id=tool_id,\n        cycle_id=cycle_id,\n        min_confidence=min_confidence,\n        base_dir=base_dir,\n    )\n    root = ensure_tools_dir(base_dir)\n    append_tools_governance(\n        root,\n        \"agent_consensus_computed\",\n        {\n            \"tool_id\": tool_id,\n            \"cycle_id\": cycle_id,\n            \"consensus_count\": len(result.get(\"consensus\", [])) if isinstance(result, dict) else 0,\n            \"uncertainty_count\": len(result.get(\"uncertainties\", [])) if isinstance(result, dict) else 0,\n            \"min_confidence\": min_confidence,\n        },\n    )\n    return result\n","numLines":59,"startLine":330,"totalLines":388}}}
